Quant View of Global Themes 
AI intact? 
 
   
 
 
AI leadership intact, while other themes   emerge 
After a remarkable run, investors seem to be questioning AI leadership amid rising 
investment, uncertain monetisation  and growing safety concerns.  However, our  iple 
Momentum rankings for    related global themes show no deterioration  with AI 
Compute Photonics and Data Centers remain  
the top ranked themes.  At the same 
time, several other themes are   emerg  
  AI adoption is creating additional security 
requirements which supports the structural case for Cybersecurity  Recent better than 
expected reported earnings for SaaS companies suggest   disruption concerns may 
 been overblown  In the last month, Gold was the best performin  global theme as 
investors focus on diversifying portfolio exposures  
Top ranked stocks within each theme 
Among the Most Important Stocks within each theme, the name that screens highest on 
Triple Momentum within AI Compute is TSMC Photonics is Zhongji Innolight A, Data 
Centers is Seagate Tech, Robotics is Siemens, Grid Modernisation is Eaton, 
Cybersecurity is Rubrik, SaaS is Snowflake, and within Gold is Zijin Mining    
Consulting, Alcohol and Luxury remain lowest ranked 
Consulting Alcoholic Beverages, and Luxury Lifestyle continue to screen lowest on 
global themes b  Triple Momentum.  Consulting has fallen     % in the last month as AI 
developments challeng  existing business models.  Alcoholic Beverages (   %) are 
underperforming as health consciousness and moderation weigh on alcohol demand 
Luxury Lifestyle (   %) was the worst performer over the last month as consumers turn 
more selective and price sensitive  The name that screens lowest  Consulting is 
Accenture in Alcohol  
verages is Kweichow Moutai, and  Luxury Lifestyle is Hermè   
Chart   Triple Momentum for Global Themes (earnings, price, news) 
Strongest for AI Compute and Photonics, weakest for Consulting and Alcoholic Beverages

Notice to Readers: 
 
The various screens identified in this report are intended to be indicative metrics only 
and may not be used for reference purposes or as a measure of performance for any 
financial instrument or contract, or otherwise relied upon by third parties for any other 
purpose, without the prior written consent of BofA Global Research. These screens were 
not created to act as a benchmark. 
 
The screens in this report are not a recommended list either individually or as a group of 
stocks. Investors should consider the fundamentals of the companies and their own 
individual circumstances/objectives before making any investment decisions. 
 
The Global News Pulse (and the regional and sectors versions of it) is intended to be an 
indicative metric only and may not be used for reference purposes or as a measure of 
performance for any financial instrument or contract, or otherwise relied upon by third 
parties for any other purpose, without the prior written consent of BofA Global Research. 
This News Pulse was not created to act as a benchmark.

Quant View of Global Themes | 17 September 2026 
 
 
Identifying stocks associated with each theme 
 
To identify stocks which potentially relate to each investment theme, we use data from 
various sources including reported revenue, news sentiment, inclusion in relevant ETFs, 
inclusion in relevant indices, stock-to-stock correlation with industry leaders, 
competitors and suppliers of industry leaders, and company descriptions. For each stock 
identified by this process, we ensure they are relevant to the theme and that the 
exposure to a theme is not inconsequential. We found this combination of multiple 
sources of information was more useful than any one source alone in identifying stocks 
associated with each theme. 
 
Chart 32: Inputs to identify stocks associated with each investment theme 
Data from various sources are used to identify stock associated with each investment theme 
 
Source : BofA Global Quantitative Strategy 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
News 
For each theme, we create a taxonomy of relevant words and products and search 
billions of news articles for stocks mentioned in relevant news.  Highlighted stocks are 
considered candidates for inclusion.  Source = RavenPack. 
Stock-to-stock correlation 
We identify the most obvious representative stocks and industry leaders within each 
theme (e.g. AI = NVIDIA, TSMC, etc.) and calculate the correlation of relative weekly 
returns over the last three years of these representative stocks to every stock globally.  


[Page 23]
 
 
Quant View of Global Themes | 17 September 2026
23
 
 
Stocks which perform in tandem with the representative stocks within each theme are 
considered candidates for inclusion.  Source = FactSet 
Revenue 
Companies which provide information on revenue exposure to products which relate to 
each theme are considered candidates for inclusion.  Source = Bloomberg 
ETFs 
We search a database of theme-specific ETFs to identify those related to each theme 
(e.g. AI = Global X Artificial Intelligence and Technology ETF).  The constituents of each 
ETF are considered candidates for inclusion.  Source = Bloomberg 
Index 
We search a database of theme-specific Indices to identify those related to each theme 
(e.g. AI = MSCI ACWI IMI Robotics & AI Index).  The constituents of each Index are 
considered candidates for inclusion.  Source = Bloomberg 
Competitors & Suppliers 
We identify the most obvious representative stocks within each theme (e.g. AI = NVIDIA, 
TSMC, etc.) and then identify the competitors and suppliers to these representative 
stocks.  These stocks are considered candidates for inclusion.  Source = Bloomberg, 
RavenPack 
Company Description 
We search the Company Description of each stock using a taxonomy of words and 
products relating to each theme.  Highlighted stocks are considered candidates for 
inclusion.  Sources = Bloomberg, FactSet 
Caveats 
It is not a straightforward exercise determining the exposure of each company to a 
particular investment theme.  It is not an exact science, so there are some limitations, 
including: 
• 
The lists of stocks may not be exhaustive because some companies may have 
exposure to an investment theme but are not identified by the above data sources 
and process 
• 
Some included stocks may have limited exposure (e.g. they may only be using AI for 
Chatbots) 
• 
Company exposure may change through time so stocks which are not currently 
relevant to a theme may be relevant in future, and stocks which are currently 
relevant to a theme may not have been relevant in the past 
• 
While a company may have exposure to a theme, it may not currently be deriving 
any revenue or explicitly declaring revenue associated with a theme and, therefore, 
not be identified by this data and process 
• 
For the analysis of News and Company Descriptions, the inclusion of companies in 
these themes may be determined by the words and products included in the theme-
specific Taxonomies which have been created based on experience and judgement 
and may not be all inclusive. 
 
 


[Page 24]
 
 
24 
Quant View of Global Themes | 17 September 2026 
 
 
Description of Triple Momentum and Positioning analysis 
 
Triple Momentum analysis 
Triple Momentum refers to our analysis which combines signals from earnings 
momentum, price momentum, and new momentum.  For each stock, earnings 
momentum is based on changing consensus earnings estimates.  Price momentum is 
calculated as the slope of daily prices over the preceding 12 months.  For news 
momentum, Natural Language Processing quantifies trends in sentiment for stock-
specific news.  Each month, we rank all stocks from 1 to 100, where 100 represents the 
strongest Triple Momentum. 
In this Quant View of Global Themes report, we calculate a simple average of the Triple 
Momentum ranks for each theme to determine ranking of each theme relative to other 
themes and sectors, and also identify the highest ranked stocks within each theme, and 
illustrate how Triple Momentum is trending through time. 
For a detailed description of our Triple Momentum analysis, refer to Triple Momentum 
Allocator report (Triple Momentum Allocator: Momentum trimmed 07 August 2026). 
 
Positioning analysis  
Our Positioning analysis monitors the aggregate positioning of thousands of long-only 
Active funds globally versus benchmark by stock, sector, country, and region.  
Additionally, for a combination of Active and Passive funds we calculate Equity Flow 
(dollar value of stocks bought and sold each month) by stock, sector, country, and region.  
We also combine the Positioning analysis with Triple Momentum to distinguish between 
Crowded stocks with positive and negative Triple Momentum, and between Under-
owned stocks with positive and negative Triple Momentum. 
In this Quant View of Global Themes report, we calculate aggregate Positioning for each 
theme to determine how long-only investors are positioned, identify the most Crowded 
and Under-owned stocks within each theme, and illustrate how investor positioning is 
changing through time. 
For a detailed description of our Positioning analysis, refer to Global Positioning in 
Stocks report (Global Positioning in Stocks: Funds reduce Semi exposure 20 July 2026).

Appendix 
 
Table 39: Themes - Short Description 
The below table lists short description on the current themes 
Theme
Short Description
Artificial Intelligence
Companies exposed to the development, enablement, and commercial deployment of AI across the full stack including AI Compute, 
AI Data & Platforms, and AI Data Centre & Infrastructure, where revenues come from enabling or monetising AI-driven outputs.
AI Compute
The processing backbone of AI - supplying the chips, accelerators, GPUs, and high-bandwidth memory that power AI training and 
inference.
AI Platforms
The cloud, data, and software platforms that make AI usable. It includes hyperscale AI platforms, foundation models, and tools used 
by developers to create AI solutions.
Data Centres
The physical backbone that supports AI - including data centres, power, servers, cooling, networking, and electrical infrastructure 
needed to run large-scale AI workloads.
Photonics
Companies involved in generating, controlling, and detecting light (photons) and turning it into practical components and systems for 
fibre-optic networks, computation/interconnect, sensing, and precision manufacturing/medicine.
Grid Modernisation
Companies enabling the expansion, digitalisation, and resilience of electricity networks through transmission infrastructure, 
transformers, distribution equipment, grid automation, smart metering, and power management technologies.
Robotics
Companies building or enabling programmable machines that sense, manipulate, move, or automate physical tasks, including 
humanoids, industrial robots, service robots, and robot-enabling automation components.
Cybersecurity
Companies enabling secure digital operations through network security, endpoint protection, identity management, cloud and data 
security, and cyber resilience solutions.
Defense
Companies generating revenue by selling military goods and services to defence customers, including weapons systems, vehicles, 
munitions, sensors, military electronics, and defence-related services.
Space
Companies exposed to the space value chain, including launch services, satellite manufacturing, satellite communications, Earth 
observation / geo-intelligence, and the ground systems and infrastructure that enable space-based services.
 
Source : BofA Global Quantitative Strategy 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
 
 
 


[Page 111]
 
 
Quant View of Global Themes | 17 September 2026
111
 
 
Table 40: Themes - Short Description (continued) 
The below table lists short description on the current themes 
Theme
Short Description
Precision Medicine
Treatment and prevention strategy that accounts for individual differences in genetics, environment, and lifestyle – delivering the right 
treatment to the right people at the right time. Its medical field moving away from one-size-fits-all to molecular-level targeting.
Energy Storage
Companies with exposure to the energy storage value chain, including battery raw materials, materials processing, cell 
manufacturing, BESS integration, and ownership or operation of assets that store electricity for later use.
Nuclear Energy
Companies exposed to the nuclear power value chain, including uranium mining, reactor technology, fuel-cycle services, key 
components, enabling infrastructure, and nuclear plant operations used to generate electricity from nuclear fission.
Renewable Energy
Companies generating or enabling energy from naturally replenished sources such as solar, wind, hydro, geothermal, bioenergy, and 
related equipment and project value chains.
Luxury Lifestyle
Companies monetising high-end branded goods and experiences where demand is driven by exclusivity, craftsmanship, heritage, 
status, and pricing power rather than functional necessity.
Alcoholic Beverages
Brewers, distillers, and vintners with leading brands that generate recurring consumer demand through alcoholic beverage production, 
premiumisation and global distribution.
Gold
Companies whose economics are directly linked to gold - typically mining, refining, royalty/streaming, or products where demand is 
tied to gold as an investment or store of value.
Rare Earths
Companies exposed to the mining, separation, refining, magnet materials, or downstream use of rare-earth elements (REEs) that are 
critical for high-tech, energy, and defence applications.
SaaS
Companies that provide software over the internet, typically generating recurring revenue through subscriptions or usage-based 
pricing.
Consulting
Companies that generate revenue mainly from expert advice and implementation services, driven by specialised human capital rather 
than software, products, or physical assets.
Quantum Computing
Companies building or enabling computing systems that use quantum-mechanical phenomena such as superposition and 
entanglement to solve problems beyond classical methods.
 
Source : BofA Global Quantitative Strategy 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
 
 
 
 
 


[Page 112]
 
 
112 
Quant View of Global Themes | 17 September 2026 
 
 
Global Quant Strategy Publications 
Global Wave 
Our regime Indicator.  The Global Wave quantifies multi-year trends in global economic 
activity and major turning points in the cycle.  The direction of the global cycle 
influences equity market performance, regional allocation, country allocation, style 
allocation, and stock selection. The Bunkers are stocks with style characteristics for a 
downturn and the Boosters are stocks for an upturn. 
Triple Momentum Allocator 
Monthly report highlighting the benefits of combining three types of momentum, namely 
Earnings Momentum, Price Momentum, and News Momentum.  Popular uses include 1) 
identifying stocks with the strongest/weakest Triple Momentum, 2) using Triple 
Momentum for Allocation (regions/sectors/countries), 3) monitoring changes in Triple 
Momentum to highlight when signals are fading and when new opportunities are 
emerging, and 4) monitoring time series in Triple Momentum to show which sectors and 
region-sectors have been in vogue at any point in time in the past. 
Global Positioning in Stocks 
Our Positioning analysis monitors the overweights and underweights of active long-only 
funds globally (AUM $38 trillion) by aggregating active positions by stock, sector, 
country, and region.  Additionally, “Equity Flow” indicates which stocks, sectors, 
countries, and regions long-only funds are buying and selling (dollar value of stocks 
bought and sold each month).  Also, the challenge is to determine which crowded trades 
may outperform and which may face performance hurdles.  We combine our positioning 
analysis with Triple Momentum to highlight which crowded positions have positive 
catalysts “Crowded Positives” and which have negative catalysts “Crowded Negatives”.  
Similarly, we screen for under-owned stocks with negative momentum “Under-owned 
Negatives” and under-owned stocks with positive momentum “Under-owned Positives”.  
There is a Global version (Global Positioning in stocks) and an Asia version (Pacific 
Positioning).  
Quant View of Global Themes 
In this regular report, we quantitatively determine which stocks have exposure to various 
Investment Themes (AI, Robotics, Space, Luxury Lifestyle, etc.).  For each theme, we 
analyze long-only positioning, valuation, and trends in Triple Momentum for 1) the 
Theme in aggregate, and 2) each stock within each Theme.  We refresh the analysis 
regularly to include emerging Investment Themes. 
High Frequency Monitor 
A weekly report monitoring high frequency data to help equity investors track short-term 
changes in market-moving data in order to swiftly identify hot topics and turning points. 
Topics include performance, earnings revisions, news, tactical indicators, commodities, 
rates, valuation, geographic revenue exposure, Triple Momentum, and Global Investment 
Themes. 
Global Contenders & Defenders 
A stock selection model based on inexpensive momentum.  This model has been running 
live since 2002.  For each stock, this report graphically shows how consensus earnings 
expectations have changed over decades versus the market, and whether recent 
earnings revisions appear to have been reflected in relative share price performance. 
Popular ways of using this analysis include 1) reviewing the Contenders (buys) and 
Defenders (Sells) lists, 2) perusing individual stock charts, 3) monitoring region, country 


[Page 113]
 
 
Quant View of Global Themes | 17 September 2026
113
 
 
and sector themes using the “quadrant charts”, and 4) reviewing stock rankings.  Includes 
some regional and country versions of the analysis. 
Quantessential Style 
Style rotation.  Different investment styles outperform at various stages of the cycle.  
This investment style analysis relates performance of seven Quantessential Styles 
(Value, Growth, Quality, Risk, Momentum, Dividend and Size) to trends in global macro 
data.  Popular uses include 1) when to buy “Deep Value”, “Growthiest of Growth”, and 
other styles, 2) identifying stocks with various style characteristics, 3) monitoring style 
performance, and 4) explaining out/under-performance due to style tilts.  The is a Global 
version (Global Quantessential Style) and an Asia Pac version (Asia Pac Quantessential 
Style). 
Global Earnings Revision Ratio 
Shows trends in earnings expectations by region, country, and sector.  An analysis of 
Asia countries and sectors is included. 
Quant Panorama 
Summary report.  In this monthly report, we present all our views and recent research on 
regional allocation, country and sector allocation, style allocation, stock selection, and 
much more.  One-stop shopping.  Various versions include “Global Quant Panorama”, 
“Asia Pac Quant Panorama”, “Japan Quant Panorama”, and “Australia Quant Panorama”. 
Global Performance Monitor 
Monthly report analyzing the performance of global regions, countries, sectors and 
identifying the major contributors to global equity market performance.  Useful for 
report writing. 
Global Fund Performance Monitor 
A monthly report enabling investors to monitor how the competition is performing.  We 
analyze the relative returns of large long-only equity funds to understand how returns 
have been affected by fund characteristics.  Funds are grouped by Region, Investment 
Style, Active/Passive, Active Share Ratio, Funds Under Management, Stock Size, Number 
of Stocks, and Portfolio Turnover.   
Global Focus Point 
Brief comments on new research and topical investment issues.  Examples include “The 
Most Important Stocks in the World”, “Tariffs Impact”,  “Narrow Breadth could Persist”, 
and “Ten reasons Investors Underperform”

BofA Securities is currently acting as strategic advisor to Sigma Lithium Corp in 
their review of strategic alternatives.

Important Disclosures 
 
      
FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating 
Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating) 
Ratings dispersion guidelines for coverage clusterR1 
Buy 
≥ 10% 
≤ 70% 
Neutral 
≥ 0% 
≤ 30% 
Underperform 
N/A 
≥ 20% 
R1Ratings dispersions may vary from time to time where BofA Global Research believes it better reflects the investment prospects of stocks in a Coverage Cluster. 
INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be secure) 
and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, sector, 
region or other classification(s). A stock’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.
